import numpy as np

#create array with elements in the range from 0 to 1e7 - 1 (integers)

B = np.arange(int(1e7))

print('A = ', A)
21/2:
def add_with_for(A,B):
    C = np.zeros(len(A)) # initialize new array
    
    for i in range(len(A)):
        C[i] = A[i] + B[i] # element-wise sum
    return C

print('C = ', add_with_for(A, B))
21/3:
C = np.add(A,B)
print('C = ', C) # Wow! So much faster!

18/83:
from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
from nltk.corpus import stopwords
from nltk.tokenize import word_tokenize
from nltk.stem import WordNetLemmatizer
import re

# Download stopwords if not already downloaded
import nltk
nltk.download('stopwords')
nltk.download('punkt')
nltk.download('wordnet')

# Initialize the lemmatizer and stopwords
lemmatizer = WordNetLemmatizer()
stop_words = set(stopwords.words('english'))

# Function to clean and preprocess the text
def clean_text(text):
    # Remove non-alphabetic characters and convert to lowercase
    text = re.sub(r'[^a-zA-Z\s]', '', text).lower()
    # Tokenize the text
    words = word_tokenize(text)
    # Remove stopwords and short words, then lemmatize
    cleaned_words = [lemmatizer.lemmatize(word) for word in words if word not in stop_words and len(word) > 2]
    return ' '.join(cleaned_words)

# Load and clean the text data
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = clean_text(pos_file.read())
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = clean_text(neg_file.read())

# Combine the texts into a list (corpus)
corpus = [pos_text, neg_text]

# Initialize the TfidfVectorizer with stop words removed
vectorizer = TfidfVectorizer()

# Fit the vectorizer to the cleaned corpus and transform the data into a TF-IDF matrix
tfidf_matrix = vectorizer.fit_transform(corpus)

# Convert the TF-IDF matrix to an array for easy viewing
tfidf_array = tfidf_matrix.toarray()

# Get the feature names (unique words)
feature_names = vectorizer.get_feature_names_out()

# Create a DataFrame for better visualization
tfidf_df = pd.DataFrame(tfidf_array, index=['Positive', 'Negative'], columns=feature_names)

# Display the top words by TF-IDF score
top_pos_words = tfidf_df.loc['Positive'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)
top_neg_words = tfidf_df.loc['Negative'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)

print("Top words in Positive Texts by TF-IDF:")
print(top_pos_words)

print("\nTop words in Negative Texts by TF-IDF:")
print(top_neg_words)

pos = []
neg = []

for row in range(len(data)):
    if data.iloc[row]["overall"] > 3:
        pos.append(data.iloc[row]["reviewText"])
    elif data.iloc[row]["overall"] < 3:
        neg.append(data.iloc[row]["reviewText"])

with open("pos.txt", 'w') as f:
    f.write("\n".join(pos))

with open("neg.txt", 'w') as f:
    f.write("\n".join(neg))
    
# Check the generated files!

from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
from nltk.corpus import stopwords
from nltk.tokenize import word_tokenize
from nltk.stem import WordNetLemmatizer
import re

# Download stopwords if not already downloaded
import nltk


# Initialize the lemmatizer and stopwords
lemmatizer = WordNetLemmatizer()
stop_words = set(stopwords.words('english'))

# Function to clean and preprocess the text
def clean_text(text):
    # Remove non-alphabetic characters and convert to lowercase
    text = re.sub(r'[^a-zA-Z\s]', '', text).lower()
    # Tokenize the text
    words = word_tokenize(text)
    # Remove stopwords and short words, then lemmatize
    cleaned_words = [lemmatizer.lemmatize(word) for word in words if word not in stop_words and len(word) > 2]
    return ' '.join(cleaned_words)

# Load and clean the text data
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = clean_text(pos_file.read())
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = clean_text(neg_file.read())

# Combine the texts into a list (corpus)
corpus = [pos_text, neg_text]

# Initialize the TfidfVectorizer with stop words removed
vectorizer = TfidfVectorizer()

# Fit the vectorizer to the cleaned corpus and transform the data into a TF-IDF matrix
tfidf_matrix = vectorizer.fit_transform(corpus)

# Convert the TF-IDF matrix to an array for easy viewing
tfidf_array = tfidf_matrix.toarray()

# Get the feature names (unique words)
feature_names = vectorizer.get_feature_names_out()

# Create a DataFrame for better visualization
tfidf_df = pd.DataFrame(tfidf_array, index=['Positive', 'Negative'], columns=feature_names)

# Display the top words by TF-IDF score
top_pos_words = tfidf_df.loc['Positive'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)
top_neg_words = tfidf_df.loc['Negative'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)

from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
from nltk.corpus import stopwords
from nltk.tokenize import word_tokenize
from nltk.stem import WordNetLemmatizer
import re

# Download stopwords if not already downloaded
import nltk


# Initialize the lemmatizer and stopwords
lemmatizer = WordNetLemmatizer()
stop_words = set(stopwords.words('english'))

# Function to clean and preprocess the text
def clean_text(text):
    # Remove non-alphabetic characters and convert to lowercase
    text = re.sub(r'[^a-zA-Z\s]', '', text).lower()
    # Tokenize the text
    words = word_tokenize(text)
    # Remove stopwords and short words, then lemmatize
    cleaned_words = [lemmatizer.lemmatize(word) for word in words if word not in stop_words and len(word) > 2]
    return ' '.join(cleaned_words)

# Load and clean the text data
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = clean_text(pos_file.read())
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = clean_text(neg_file.read())

# Combine the texts into a list (corpus)
corpus = [pos_text, neg_text]

# Initialize the TfidfVectorizer with stop words removed
vectorizer = TfidfVectorizer()

# Fit the vectorizer to the cleaned corpus and transform the data into a TF-IDF matrix
tfidf_matrix = vectorizer.fit_transform(corpus)

# Convert the TF-IDF matrix to an array for easy viewing
tfidf_array = tfidf_matrix.toarray()

# Get the feature names (unique words)
feature_names = vectorizer.get_feature_names()

# Create a DataFrame for better visualization
tfidf_df = pd.DataFrame(tfidf_array, index=['Positive', 'Negative'], columns=feature_names)

# Display the top words by TF-IDF score
top_pos_words = tfidf_df.loc['Positive'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)
top_neg_words = tfidf_df.loc['Negative'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)

print("Top words in Positive Texts by TF-IDF:")
print(top_pos_words)

print("\nTop words in Negative Texts by TF-IDF:")
print(top_neg_words)

from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
import re

# Function to clean and preprocess the text
def clean_text(text):
    # Remove non-alphabetic characters
    text = re.sub(r'[^a-zA-Z\s]', '', text)
    # Lowercase the text
    text = text.lower()
    return text

# Load and clean the text data
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = clean_text(pos_file.read())
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = clean_text(neg_file.read())

# Combine the texts into a list (corpus)
corpus = [pos_text, neg_text]

# Initialize the TfidfVectorizer with stop words removed
vectorizer = TfidfVectorizer(stop_words='english')

# Fit the vectorizer to the cleaned corpus and transform the data into a TF-IDF matrix
tfidf_matrix = vectorizer.fit_transform(corpus)

# Convert the TF-IDF matrix to an array for easy viewing
tfidf_array = tfidf_matrix.toarray()

# Get the feature names (unique words)
feature_names = vectorizer.get_feature_names()

# Create a DataFrame for better visualization
tfidf_df = pd.DataFrame(tfidf_array, index=['Positive', 'Negative'], columns=feature_names)

# Display the TF-IDF matrix
print("TF-IDF Matrix:")
print(tfidf_df)

from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
import pandas as pd

# Load the text data from pos.txt and neg.txt files
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = pos_file.read()
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = neg_file.read()

# Combine the texts into a list (corpus)
corpus = [pos_text, neg_text]

# Initialize the TfidfVectorizer
vectorizer = TfidfVectorizer()

# Fit the vectorizer to the corpus and transform the data into a TF-IDF matrix
tfidf_matrix = vectorizer.fit_transform(corpus)

# Convert the TF-IDF matrix to an array for easy viewing
tfidf_array = tfidf_matrix.toarray()

# Get the feature names (unique words) using get_feature_names()
feature_names = vectorizer.get_feature_names()

# Create a DataFrame for better visualization
tfidf_df = pd.DataFrame(tfidf_array, index=['Positive', 'Negative'], columns=feature_names)

# Display the TF-IDF matrix
print("TF-IDF Matrix:")
print(tfidf_df)
18/88:
# Assuming tfidf_df is your TF-IDF DataFrame
# Get the number of rows and columns
num_rows, num_columns = tfidf_df.shape

# Calculate the middle indices
middle_row_start = num_rows // 2 - 1
middle_row_end = middle_row_start + 2  # Show 2 rows around the middle
middle_col_start = num_columns // 2 - 5
middle_col_end = middle_col_start + 10  # Show 10 columns around the middle

# Ensure the indices are within bounds
middle_row_start = max(middle_row_start, 0)
middle_row_end = min(middle_row_end, num_rows)
middle_col_start = max(middle_col_start, 0)
middle_col_end = min(middle_col_end, num_columns)

# Extract the middle portion of the TF-IDF matrix
tfidf_middle_df = tfidf_df.iloc[middle_row_start:middle_row_end, middle_col_start:middle_col_end]

# Display the middle portion
print(tfidf_middle_df)

from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
from nltk.corpus import stopwords
from nltk.tokenize import word_tokenize
from nltk.stem import WordNetLemmatizer
import re

# Download stopwords if not already downloaded
import nltk


# Initialize the lemmatizer and stopwords
lemmatizer = WordNetLemmatizer()
stop_words = set(stopwords.words('english'))

# Function to clean and preprocess the text
def clean_text(text):
    # Remove non-alphabetic characters and convert to lowercase
    text = re.sub(r'[^a-zA-Z\s]', '', text).lower()
    # Tokenize the text
    words = word_tokenize(text)
    # Remove stopwords and short words, then lemmatize
    cleaned_words = [lemmatizer.lemmatize(word) for word in words if word not in stop_words and len(word) > 2]
    return ' '.join(cleaned_words)

# Load and clean the text data
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = clean_text(pos_file.read())
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = clean_text(neg_file.read())

# Combine the texts into a list (corpus)
corpus = [pos_text, neg_text]

# Initialize the TfidfVectorizer with stop words removed
vectorizer = TfidfVectorizer()

# Fit the vectorizer to the cleaned corpus and transform the data into a TF-IDF matrix
tfidf_matrix = vectorizer.fit_transform(corpus)

# Convert the TF-IDF matrix to an array for easy viewing
tfidf_array = tfidf_matrix.toarray()

# Get the feature names (unique words)
feature_names = vectorizer.get_feature_names()

# Create a DataFrame for better visualization
tfidf_df = pd.DataFrame(tfidf_array, index=['Positive', 'Negative'], columns=feature_names)

# Display the top words by TF-IDF score
top_pos_words = tfidf_df.loc['Positive'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)
top_neg_words = tfidf_df.loc['Negative'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)

print("Top words in Positive Texts by TF-IDF:")
print(top_pos_words)

print("\nTop words in Negative Texts by TF-IDF:")
print(top_neg_words)

# Extract the first letter from the `Cabin` column as the deck letter
df['Deck'] = df['Cabin'].str.extract('([A-Z])', expand=False)

# Drop the original `Cabin` column
df.drop('Cabin', axis=1, inplace=True)

# Print the unique deck letters to verify
print("Unique deck letters:", df['Deck'].unique())
22/4:
# Use this cell for your code
print(df.head())

# List of categorical columns to encode
categorical_columns = ['Sex', 'Embarked','Deck','Pclass', 'SibSp','Survived','Parch']

# Apply label encoding using factorize for each categorical column
for column in categorical_columns:
    df[column] = pd.factorize(df[column])[0]

# Display the first few rows of the DataFrame to see the changes
print(df.head())

print(df['Deck'].unique())
22/5:
# Use this cell for your code

# Identifying columns with missing values
print(df.head())



# List of continuous and categorical columns (after label encoding)
continuous_columns = ['Age', 'Fare']
categorical_columns = ['Survived', 'Pclass', 'Sex', 'SibSp', 'Parch', 'Embarked', 'Deck']

# Impute missing values for continuous variables with the mean
for column in continuous_columns:
    df[column].fillna(df[column].mean(), inplace=True)

# Impute missing values for categorical variables with the mode
for column in categorical_columns:
    mode_value = df[column][df[column] != -1].mode()[0]  # Calculate the mode excluding -1
    df[column] = df[column].replace(-1, mode_value) 
# Display the first few rows to verify the imputation
print(df.head())

# Verify that there are no missing values left
print("\nMissing values after imputation:\n", df.isnull().sum())

print(df.head())

print(df['Deck'].unique())

import pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

# Load the JSON file into a DataFrame
file_path = 'titanic_modified.json'

plt.ylabel('Survival Rate')

plt.title('Survival by Age')
plt.xlabel('Age')
plt.ylabel('Count')
plt.legend()

# Categorical variables in the dataset
categorical_columns = ['Survived', 'Pclass', 'Sex', 'SibSp', 'Parch', 'Embarked', 'Deck']

# Numerical variables in the dataset
numerical_columns = ['Age', 'Fare']

# Dictionary to store the mode of categorical variables
categorical_modes = {}
for column in categorical_columns:
    mode_value = df[column].mode()[0]
    categorical_modes[column] = mode_value

# Dictionary to store the median of numerical variables
numerical_medians = {}
for column in numerical_columns:
    median_value = df[column].median()
    numerical_medians[column] = median_value

# Print the results
print("Modes of Categorical Variables:")
for column, mode in categorical_modes.items():
    print(f"{column}: {mode}")

print("\nMedians of Numerical Variables:")
for column, median in numerical_medians.items():
    print(f"{column}: {median}")
22/10:
# Split the dataset into survivors and non-survivors
survivors = df[df['Survived'] == 1]
non_survivors = df[df['Survived'] == 0]

# Initialize dictionaries to store modes and medians
survivor_modes = {}
survivor_medians = {}
non_survivor_modes = {}
non_survivor_medians = {}

# Calculate modes for categorical variables and medians for numerical variables for survivors
for column in categorical_columns:
    if column != 'Survived':  # Skip 'Survived' as it's the target variable
        survivor_modes[column] = survivors[column].mode()[0]
for column in numerical_columns:
    survivor_medians[column] = survivors[column].median()

# Calculate modes for categorical variables and medians for numerical variables for non-survivors
for column in categorical_columns:
    if column != 'Survived':  # Skip 'Survived' as it's the target variable
        non_survivor_modes[column] = non_survivors[column].mode()[0]
for column in numerical_columns:
    non_survivor_medians[column] = non_survivors[column].median()

# Combine modes and medians to form the "average survivor" and "average non-survivor"
average_survivor = {}
average_survivor.update(survivor_modes)
average_survivor.update(survivor_medians)

average_non_survivor = {}
average_non_survivor.update(non_survivor_modes)
average_non_survivor.update(non_survivor_medians)

# Print the results
print("Average Survivor:")
for feature, value in average_survivor.items():
    print(f"{feature}: {value}")

print("\nAverage Non-Survivor:")
for feature, value in average_non_survivor.items():
    print(f"{feature}: {value}")

# Use this cell for your code

# Visualizing distributions using histograms and box plots

# Plot histograms for numerical variables
numerical_variables = ['Age', 'Fare']
for column in numerical_variables:
    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 4))
    plt.hist(survivors[column], bins=20, alpha=0.5, label='Survivors', color='green')
    plt.hist(non_survivors[column], bins=20, alpha=0.5, label='Non-Survivors', color='red')
    plt.title(f'Distribution of {column} for Survivors and Non-Survivors')
    plt.xlabel(column)
    plt.ylabel('Frequency')
    plt.legend()
    plt.show()

# Plot box plots for numerical variables
for column in numerical_variables:
    plt.figure(figsize=(8, 4))
    plt.boxplot([survivors[column], non_survivors[column]], labels=['Survivors', 'Non-Survivors'])
    plt.title(f'Box Plot of {column} for Survivors and Non-Survivors')
    plt.ylabel(column)
    plt.show()

# Finding passengers similar to the average representative
# Combine all the columns for numerical comparison
all_features = numerical_columns + [col for col in categorical_columns if col != 'Survived']

# Convert average survivor and non-survivor into arrays for comparison
average_survivor_values = np.array([average_survivor[feature] for feature in all_features])
average_non_survivor_values = np.array([average_non_survivor[feature] for feature in all_features])

# Function to calculate Euclidean distance
def euclidean_distance(row, average_values):
    row_values = row[all_features].values
    return np.sqrt(np.sum((row_values - average_values) ** 2))

# Find passengers similar to the average survivor
df['dist_to_avg_survivor'] = df.apply(lambda row: euclidean_distance(row, average_survivor_values), axis=1)
df['dist_to_avg_non_survivor'] = df.apply(lambda row: euclidean_distance(row, average_non_survivor_values), axis=1)

# Closest passenger to average survivor
closest_to_avg_survivor = df[df['dist_to_avg_survivor'] == df['dist_to_avg_survivor'].min()]
# Closest passenger to average non-survivor
closest_to_avg_non_survivor = df[df['dist_to_avg_non_survivor'] == df['dist_to_avg_non_survivor'].min()]

# Print closest passengers
print("Passenger closest to average survivor:")
print(closest_to_avg_survivor)

print("\nPassenger closest to average non-survivor:")
print(closest_to_avg_non_survivor)

# Use this cell for your code

# Identifying columns with missing values
print(df.head())



# List of continuous and categorical columns (after label encoding)
continuous_columns = ['Age', 'Fare']
categorical_columns = ['Survived', 'Pclass', 'Sex', 'SibSp', 'Parch', 'Embarked', 'Deck']

# Impute missing values for continuous variables with the mean
for column in continuous_columns:
    df[column].fillna(df[column].mean(), inplace=True)

# Impute missing values for categorical variables with the mode
for column in categorical_columns:
    mode_value = df[column][df[column] != -1].mode()[0]  # Calculate the mode excluding -1
    df[column] = df[column].replace(-1, mode_value) 
# Display the first few rows to verify the imputation
print(df.head())

# Verify that there are no missing values left
print("\nMissing values after imputation:\n", df.isnull().sum())

print(df.head())

print(df['Deck'].unique())

# Use this cell for your code

from collections import Counter
import string

# Function to preprocess text: lowercase, remove punctuation, and split into words
def preprocess_text(text):
    # Convert to lowercase
    text = text.lower()
    # Remove punctuation
    text = text.translate(str.maketrans('', '', string.punctuation))
    # Split into words
    words = text.split()
    return words

# Load the text data from pos.txt and neg.txt files
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = pos_file.read()
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = neg_file.read()

# Preprocess the text
pos_words = preprocess_text(pos_text)
neg_words = preprocess_text(neg_text)

# Find the most common words
pos_common_words = Counter(pos_words).most_common(10)
neg_common_words = Counter(neg_words).most_common(10)

# Print the results
print("Most Common Words in Positive Texts:")
for word, freq in pos_common_words:
    print(f"{word}: {freq}")

print("\nMost Common Words in Negative Texts:")
for word, freq in neg_common_words:
    print(f"{word}: {freq}")
22/25:
from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
import pandas as pd

# Load the text data from pos.txt and neg.txt files
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = pos_file.read()
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = neg_file.read()

# Combine the texts into a list (corpus)
corpus = [pos_text, neg_text]

# Initialize the TfidfVectorizer
vectorizer = TfidfVectorizer()

# Fit the vectorizer to the corpus and transform the data into a TF-IDF matrix
tfidf_matrix = vectorizer.fit_transform(corpus)

# Convert the TF-IDF matrix to an array for easy viewing
tfidf_array = tfidf_matrix.toarray()

# Get the feature names (unique words) using get_feature_names()
feature_names = vectorizer.get_feature_names()

# Create a DataFrame for better visualization
tfidf_df = pd.DataFrame(tfidf_array, index=['Positive', 'Negative'], columns=feature_names)

# Display the TF-IDF matrix
print("TF-IDF Matrix:")
print(tfidf_df)
22/26:
from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
from nltk.corpus import stopwords
from nltk.tokenize import word_tokenize
from nltk.stem import WordNetLemmatizer
import re

# Download stopwords if not already downloaded
import nltk


# Initialize the lemmatizer and stopwords
lemmatizer = WordNetLemmatizer()
stop_words = set(stopwords.words('english'))

# Function to clean and preprocess the text
def clean_text(text):
    # Remove non-alphabetic characters and convert to lowercase
    text = re.sub(r'[^a-zA-Z\s]', '', text).lower()
    # Tokenize the text
    words = word_tokenize(text)
    # Remove stopwords and short words, then lemmatize
    cleaned_words = [lemmatizer.lemmatize(word) for word in words if word not in stop_words and len(word) > 2]
    return ' '.join(cleaned_words)

# Load and clean the text data
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = clean_text(pos_file.read())
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = clean_text(neg_file.read())

# Combine the texts into a list (corpus)
corpus = [pos_text, neg_text]

# Initialize the TfidfVectorizer with stop words removed
vectorizer = TfidfVectorizer()

# Fit the vectorizer to the cleaned corpus and transform the data into a TF-IDF matrix
tfidf_matrix = vectorizer.fit_transform(corpus)

# Convert the TF-IDF matrix to an array for easy viewing
tfidf_array = tfidf_matrix.toarray()

# Get the feature names (unique words)
feature_names = vectorizer.get_feature_names()

# Create a DataFrame for better visualization
tfidf_df = pd.DataFrame(tfidf_array, index=['Positive', 'Negative'], columns=feature_names)

# Display the top words by TF-IDF score
top_pos_words = tfidf_df.loc['Positive'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)
top_neg_words = tfidf_df.loc['Negative'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)

print("Top words in Positive Texts by TF-IDF:")
print(top_pos_words)

print("\nTop words in Negative Texts by TF-IDF:")
print(top_neg_words)
22/27:
from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
import re

# Function to clean and preprocess the text
def clean_text(text):
    # Remove non-alphabetic characters
    text = re.sub(r'[^a-zA-Z\s]', '', text)
    # Lowercase the text
    text = text.lower()
    return text

# Load and clean the text data
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = clean_text(pos_file.read())
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = clean_text(neg_file.read())

# Combine the texts into a list (corpus)
corpus = [pos_text, neg_text]

# Initialize the TfidfVectorizer with stop words removed
vectorizer = TfidfVectorizer(stop_words='english')

# Fit the vectorizer to the cleaned corpus and transform the data into a TF-IDF matrix
tfidf_matrix = vectorizer.fit_transform(corpus)

# Convert the TF-IDF matrix to an array for easy viewing
tfidf_array = tfidf_matrix.toarray()

# Get the feature names (unique words)
feature_names = vectorizer.get_feature_names()

# Create a DataFrame for better visualization
tfidf_df = pd.DataFrame(tfidf_array, index=['Positive', 'Negative'], columns=feature_names)

# Display the TF-IDF matrix
print("TF-IDF Matrix:")
print(tfidf_df)

# Display the top words
top_pos_words, top_neg_words

from wordcloud import WordCloud
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

# Generate word clouds for positive and negative texts
def generate_wordcloud(data, title):
    wordcloud = WordCloud(width=800, height=400, background_color='white').generate_from_frequencies(data)
    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 5))
    plt.imshow(wordcloud, interpolation='bilinear')
    plt.title(title)
    plt.axis('off')
    plt.show()

# Extract words and their scores for the word cloud
top_pos_words_dict = top_pos_words.to_dict()
top_neg_words_dict = top_neg_words.to_dict()

# Generate word clouds
generate_wordcloud(top_pos_words_dict, 'Top Words in Positive Texts by TF-IDF')
generate_wordcloud(top_neg_words_dict, 'Top Words in Negative Texts by TF-IDF')

from wordcloud import WordCloud
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

# Generate word clouds for positive and negative texts
def generate_wordcloud(data, title):
    wordcloud = WordCloud(width=800, height=400, background_color='white').generate_from_frequencies(data)
    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 5))
    plt.imshow(wordcloud, interpolation='bilinear')
    plt.title(title)
    plt.axis('off')
    plt.show()

# Extract words and their scores for the word cloud

# Use this cell for your code

# Identifying columns with missing values
print(df.head())



# List of continuous and categorical columns (after label encoding)
continuous_columns = ['Age', 'Fare']
categorical_columns = ['Survived', 'Pclass', 'Sex', 'SibSp', 'Parch', 'Embarked', 'Deck']

# Impute missing values for continuous variables with the mean
for column in continuous_columns:
    df[column].fillna(df[column].mean(), inplace=True)

# Impute missing values for categorical variables with the mode
for column in categorical_columns:
    mode_value = df[column][df[column] != -1].mode()[0]  # Calculate the mode excluding -1
    df[column] = df[column].replace(-1, mode_value) 
# Display the first few rows to verify the imputation
print(df.head())

# Verify that there are no missing values left
print("\nMissing values after imputation:\n", df.isnull().sum())

print(df.head())

print(df['Deck'].unique())

import pandas as pd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

# Load the JSON file into a DataFrame
file_path = 'titanic_modified.json'
df = pd.read_json(file_path)

# Plot 1: Survival by Gender
survival_by_gender = df.groupby('Sex')['Survived'].mean()
plt.figure(figsize=(8, 6))
survival_by_gender.plot(kind='bar')
plt.title('Survival Rate by Gender')
plt.xlabel('Gender (0 = Male, 1 = Female)')
plt.ylabel('Survival Rate')
plt.show()

# Plot 2: Survival by Passenger Class
survival_by_class = df.groupby('Pclass')['Survived'].mean()
plt.figure(figsize=(8, 6))
survival_by_class.plot(kind='bar', color='orange')
plt.title('Survival Rate by Passenger Class')
plt.xlabel('Passenger Class (0 = Third, 1 = First, 2 = Second)')
plt.ylabel('Survival Rate')
plt.show()

# Plot 3: Survival by Age (Histogram)
plt.figure(figsize=(8, 6))
plt.hist(df[df['Survived'] == 1]['Age'], bins=30, alpha=0.5, label='Survived')
plt.hist(df[df['Survived'] == 0]['Age'], bins=30, alpha=0.5, label='Not Survived')
plt.title('Survival by Age')
plt.xlabel('Age')
plt.ylabel('Count')
plt.legend()
plt.show()

# Plot 4: Survival by Fare (Histogram)
plt.figure(figsize=(8, 6))
plt.hist(df[df['Survived'] == 1]['Fare'], bins=30, alpha=0.5, label='Survived')
plt.hist(df[df['Survived'] == 0]['Fare'], bins=30, alpha=0.5, label='Not Survived')
plt.title('Survival by Fare')
plt.xlabel('Fare')
plt.ylabel('Count')
plt.legend()
plt.show()

# Use this cell for your code

# Categorical variables in the dataset
categorical_columns = ['Survived', 'Pclass', 'Sex', 'SibSp', 'Parch', 'Embarked', 'Deck']

# Numerical variables in the dataset
numerical_columns = ['Age', 'Fare']

# Dictionary to store the mode of categorical variables
categorical_modes = {}
for column in categorical_columns:
    mode_value = df[column].mode()[0]
    categorical_modes[column] = mode_value

# Dictionary to store the median of numerical variables
numerical_medians = {}
for column in numerical_columns:
    median_value = df[column].median()
    numerical_medians[column] = median_value

# Print the results
print("Modes of Categorical Variables:")
for column, mode in categorical_modes.items():
    print(f"{column}: {mode}")

print("\nMedians of Numerical Variables:")
for column, median in numerical_medians.items():
    print(f"{column}: {median}")
23/10:
# Split the dataset into survivors and non-survivors
survivors = df[df['Survived'] == 1]
non_survivors = df[df['Survived'] == 0]

# Initialize dictionaries to store modes and medians
survivor_modes = {}
survivor_medians = {}
non_survivor_modes = {}
non_survivor_medians = {}

# Calculate modes for categorical variables and medians for numerical variables for survivors
for column in categorical_columns:
    if column != 'Survived':  # Skip 'Survived' as it's the target variable
        survivor_modes[column] = survivors[column].mode()[0]
for column in numerical_columns:
    survivor_medians[column] = survivors[column].median()

# Calculate modes for categorical variables and medians for numerical variables for non-survivors
for column in categorical_columns:
    if column != 'Survived':  # Skip 'Survived' as it's the target variable
        non_survivor_modes[column] = non_survivors[column].mode()[0]
for column in numerical_columns:
    non_survivor_medians[column] = non_survivors[column].median()

# Combine modes and medians to form the "average survivor" and "average non-survivor"
average_survivor = {}
average_survivor.update(survivor_modes)
average_survivor.update(survivor_medians)

average_non_survivor = {}
average_non_survivor.update(non_survivor_modes)
average_non_survivor.update(non_survivor_medians)

# Print the results
print("Average Survivor:")
for feature, value in average_survivor.items():
    print(f"{feature}: {value}")

print("\nAverage Non-Survivor:")
for feature, value in average_non_survivor.items():
    print(f"{feature}: {value}")

# Use this cell for your code
import numpy as np
# Visualizing distributions using histograms and box plots

# Plot histograms for numerical variables
numerical_variables = ['Age', 'Fare']
for column in numerical_variables:
    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 4))
    plt.hist(survivors[column], bins=20, alpha=0.5, label='Survivors', color='green')
    plt.hist(non_survivors[column], bins=20, alpha=0.5, label='Non-Survivors', color='red')
    plt.title(f'Distribution of {column} for Survivors and Non-Survivors')
    plt.xlabel(column)
    plt.ylabel('Frequency')
    plt.legend()
    plt.show()

# Plot box plots for numerical variables
for column in numerical_variables:
    plt.figure(figsize=(8, 4))
    plt.boxplot([survivors[column], non_survivors[column]], labels=['Survivors', 'Non-Survivors'])
    plt.title(f'Box Plot of {column} for Survivors and Non-Survivors')
    plt.ylabel(column)
    plt.show()

# Finding passengers similar to the average representative
# Combine all the columns for numerical comparison
all_features = numerical_columns + [col for col in categorical_columns if col != 'Survived']

# Convert average survivor and non-survivor into arrays for comparison
average_survivor_values = np.array([average_survivor[feature] for feature in all_features])
average_non_survivor_values = np.array([average_non_survivor[feature] for feature in all_features])

# Function to calculate Euclidean distance
def euclidean_distance(row, average_values):
    row_values = row[all_features].values
    return np.sqrt(np.sum((row_values - average_values) ** 2))

# Find passengers similar to the average survivor
df['dist_to_avg_survivor'] = df.apply(lambda row: euclidean_distance(row, average_survivor_values), axis=1)
df['dist_to_avg_non_survivor'] = df.apply(lambda row: euclidean_distance(row, average_non_survivor_values), axis=1)

# Closest passenger to average survivor
closest_to_avg_survivor = df[df['dist_to_avg_survivor'] == df['dist_to_avg_survivor'].min()]
# Closest passenger to average non-survivor
closest_to_avg_non_survivor = df[df['dist_to_avg_non_survivor'] == df['dist_to_avg_non_survivor'].min()]

# Print closest passengers
print("Passenger closest to average survivor:")
print(closest_to_avg_survivor)

print("\nPassenger closest to average non-survivor:")
print(closest_to_avg_non_survivor)

# Use this cell for your code

from collections import Counter
import string

# Function to preprocess text: lowercase, remove punctuation, and split into words
def preprocess_text(text):
    # Convert to lowercase
    text = text.lower()
    # Remove punctuation
    text = text.translate(str.maketrans('', '', string.punctuation))
    # Split into words
    words = text.split()
    return words

# Load the text data from pos.txt and neg.txt files
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = pos_file.read()
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = neg_file.read()

# Preprocess the text
pos_words = preprocess_text(pos_text)
neg_words = preprocess_text(neg_text)

# Find the most common words
pos_common_words = Counter(pos_words).most_common(10)
neg_common_words = Counter(neg_words).most_common(10)

# Print the results
print("Most Common Words in Positive Texts:")
for word, freq in pos_common_words:
    print(f"{word}: {freq}")

print("\nMost Common Words in Negative Texts:")
for word, freq in neg_common_words:
    print(f"{word}: {freq}")
23/14:
from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
import pandas as pd

# Load the text data from pos.txt and neg.txt files
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = pos_file.read()
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = neg_file.read()

# Combine the texts into a list (corpus)
corpus = [pos_text, neg_text]

# Initialize the TfidfVectorizer
vectorizer = TfidfVectorizer()

# Fit the vectorizer to the corpus and transform the data into a TF-IDF matrix
tfidf_matrix = vectorizer.fit_transform(corpus)

# Convert the TF-IDF matrix to an array for easy viewing
tfidf_array = tfidf_matrix.toarray()

# Get the feature names (unique words) using get_feature_names()
feature_names = vectorizer.get_feature_names()

# Create a DataFrame for better visualization
tfidf_df = pd.DataFrame(tfidf_array, index=['Positive', 'Negative'], columns=feature_names)

# Display the TF-IDF matrix
print("TF-IDF Matrix:")
print(tfidf_df)
23/15:
# Use this cell for your code
# Extract the top 10 words with the highest TF-IDF scores for each class
top_pos_words = tfidf_df.loc['Positive'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)
top_neg_words = tfidf_df.loc['Negative'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)

# Display the top words
top_pos_words, top_neg_words

# Adding jitter and hue for survival status
sns.pairplot(df, vars=['Age', 'Fare'], hue='Survived', palette={0: 'red', 1: 'green'}, plot_kws={'alpha': 0.5, 's': 50, 'edgecolor': 'k'})
plt.show()

# Use this cell for your code

from collections import Counter
import string

# Function to preprocess text: lowercase, remove punctuation, and split into words
def preprocess_text(text):
    # Convert to lowercase
    text = text.lower()
    # Remove punctuation
    text = text.translate(str.maketrans('', '', string.punctuation))
    # Split into words
    words = text.split()
    return words

# Load the text data from pos.txt and neg.txt files
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = pos_file.read()
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = neg_file.read()

# Preprocess the text
pos_words = preprocess_text(pos_text)
neg_words = preprocess_text(neg_text)

# Find the most common words
pos_common_words = Counter(pos_words).most_common(10)
neg_common_words = Counter(neg_words).most_common(10)

# Print the results
print("Most Common Words in Positive Texts:")
for word, freq in pos_common_words:
    print(f"{word}: {freq}")

print("\nMost Common Words in Negative Texts:")
for word, freq in neg_common_words:
    print(f"{word}: {freq}")
23/31:
from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
import pandas as pd

# Load the text data from pos.txt and neg.txt files
with open('pos.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as pos_file:
    pos_text = pos_file.read()
with open('neg.txt', 'r', encoding='utf-8') as neg_file:
    neg_text = neg_file.read()

# Combine the texts into a list (corpus)
corpus = [pos_text, neg_text]

# Initialize the TfidfVectorizer
vectorizer = TfidfVectorizer()

# Fit the vectorizer to the corpus and transform the data into a TF-IDF matrix
tfidf_matrix = vectorizer.fit_transform(corpus)

# Convert the TF-IDF matrix to an array for easy viewing
tfidf_array = tfidf_matrix.toarray()

# Get the feature names (unique words) using get_feature_names()
feature_names = vectorizer.get_feature_names()

# Create a DataFrame for better visualization
tfidf_df = pd.DataFrame(tfidf_array, index=['Positive', 'Negative'], columns=feature_names)

# Display the TF-IDF matrix
print("TF-IDF Matrix:")
print(tfidf_df)
23/32:
# Use this cell for your code
# Extract the top 10 words with the highest TF-IDF scores for each class
top_pos_words = tfidf_df.loc['Positive'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)
top_neg_words = tfidf_df.loc['Negative'].sort_values(ascending=False).head(10)

# Display the top words
top_pos_words, top_neg_words
23/33:
import WordCloud
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

# Generate word clouds for positive and negative texts
def generate_wordcloud(data, title):
    wordcloud = WordCloud(width=800, height=400, background_color='white').generate_from_frequencies(data)
    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 5))
    plt.imshow(wordcloud, interpolation='bilinear')
    plt.title(title)
    plt.axis('off')
    plt.show()

# Extract words and their scores for the word cloud
top_pos_words_dict = top_pos_words.to_dict()
top_neg_words_dict = top_neg_words.to_dict()

# Generate word clouds
generate_wordcloud(top_pos_words_dict, 'Top Words in Positive Texts by TF-IDF')
generate_wordcloud(top_neg_words_dict, 'Top Words in Negative Texts by TF-IDF')

import string

# Function to preprocess text: lowercase, remove punctuation, and split into words
def preprocess_text(text):
    # Convert to lowercase
    text = text.lower()
    # Remove punctuation

    # Split into words

top_neg_words_dict = top_neg_words.to_dict()

# Use this cell for your code

import geopandas as gpd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

# Load world data
world = gpd.read_file('path_to_extracted/world_m.shp')

# Load cities data
cities = gpd.read_file('path_to_extracted/cities.shp')

# Check the coordinate systems
print("World CRS:", world.crs)
print("Cities CRS:", cities.crs)

# Reproject cities data if CRS are different
if world.crs != cities.crs:
    cities = cities.to_crs(world.crs)

# Plot the data
fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(10, 10))

# Plot world data
world.plot(ax=ax, color='lightgrey')

# Plot cities data
cities.plot(ax=ax, color='red', markersize=5)

plt.title('World Map with Cities')
plt.xlabel('Longitude')
plt.ylabel('Latitude')
plt.show()

# Use this cell for your code
# Import necessary libraries
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import os

# Path to the CSV file and image folder
csv_path = 'C:\Users\S\Downloads\New folder (8)\verification-task\train.csv'
image_folder = 'C:\Users\S\Downloads\New folder (8)\hasy-data\'

# Read the labels CSV
labels_df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)

# Filter rows where symbol_id is between 70 and 79 (inclusive)
filtered_labels_df = labels_df[(labels_df['symbol_id'] >= 70) & (labels_df['symbol_id'] <= 79)]

# Initialize lists to store the flattened images and corresponding labels
images_list = []
labels_list = []

# Process each image corresponding to the filtered labels
for index, row in filtered_labels_df.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join(image_folder, row['path'])
    
    # Open the image, convert to grayscale (if necessary), and resize to 32x32
    img = Image.open(image_path).convert('L')  # Convert to grayscale ('L' mode)
    
    # Flatten the image into a 1D vector (32x32 = 1024)
    img_array = np.array(img).flatten()
    
    # Append the flattened image and label to the respective lists
    images_list.append(img_array)
    labels_list.append(row['symbol_id'])

# Convert the lists to numpy arrays
X = np.array(images_list)  # Input data of shape (1020, 1024)
y = np.array(labels_list)  # Labels of shape (1020,)

# Check the shapes to ensure correctness
print("Shape of input data (X):", X.shape)
print("Shape of labels (y):", y.shape)

# Process each image corresponding to the filtered labels
for index, row in filtered_labels_df.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join(image_folder, row['path'])
    
    # Open the image, convert to grayscale (if necessary), and resize to 32x32
    img = Image.open(image_path).convert('L')  # Convert to grayscale ('L' mode)
    
    # Flatten the image into a 1D vector (32x32 = 1024)
    img_array = np.array(img).flatten()
    
    # Append the flattened image and label to the respective lists
    images_list.append(img_array)
    labels_list.append(row['symbol_id'])

# Convert the lists to numpy arrays
X = np.array(images_list)  # Input data of shape (1020, 1024)
y = np.array(labels_list)  # Labels of shape (1020,)

# Check the shapes to ensure correctness
print("Shape of input data (X):", X.shape)
print("Shape of labels (y):", y.shape)

import pandas as pd

from PIL import Image
import os

# Path to the CSV file and image folder

image_folder = 'path_to_your_extracted_data/hasy_data/'

# Read the labels CSV
labels_df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)

# Filter rows where symbol_id is between 70 and 79 (inclusive)
filtered_labels_df = labels_df[(labels_df['symbol_id'] >= 70) & (labels_df['symbol_id'] <= 79)]
print("Number of rows with symbol_id between 70 and 79:", filtered_labels_df.shape[0])
# Initialize lists to store the flattened images and corresponding labels
images_list = []
labels_list = []

# Process each image corresponding to the filtered labels
for index, row in filtered_labels_df.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join(image_folder, row['path'])
    
    # Open the image, convert to grayscale (if necessary), and resize to 32x32
    img = Image.open(image_path).convert('L')  # Convert to grayscale ('L' mode)
    
    # Flatten the image into a 1D vector (32x32 = 1024)
    img_array = np.array(img).flatten()
    
    # Append the flattened image and label to the respective lists
    images_list.append(img_array)
    labels_list.append(row['symbol_id'])

# Convert the lists to numpy arrays
X = np.array(images_list)  # Input data of shape (1020, 1024)
y = np.array(labels_list)  # Labels of shape (1020,)

# Check the shapes to ensure correctness
print("Shape of input data (X):", X.shape)
print("Shape of labels (y):", y.shape)

# Use this cell for your code
# Import necessary libraries
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import os

# Path to the CSV file and image folder
csv_path = r'C:\Users\S\Downloads\New folder (8)\verification-task\train.csv'
image_folder = r'C:\Users\S\Downloads\New folder (8)\hasy-data\\'

# Read the labels CSV
labels_df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)

# Filter rows where symbol_id is between 70 and 79 (inclusive)
filtered_labels_df = labels_df[(labels_df['symbol_id'] ) & (labels_df['symbol_id'] )]
print("Number of rows with symbol_id between 70 and 79:", filtered_labels_df.shape[0])
# Initialize lists to store the flattened images and corresponding labels
images_list = []
labels_list = []

missing_images = []
for index, row in filtered_labels_df.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join(image_folder, row['path'])
    if not os.path.exists(image_path):
        missing_images.append(image_path)

print("Number of missing images:", len(missing_images))
if missing_images:
    print("Missing image paths:", missing_images)

# Process each image corresponding to the filtered labels
for index, row in filtered_labels_df.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join(image_folder, row['path'])
    
    # Open the image, convert to grayscale (if necessary), and resize to 32x32
    img = Image.open(image_path).convert('L')  # Convert to grayscale ('L' mode)
    
    # Flatten the image into a 1D vector (32x32 = 1024)
    img_array = np.array(img).flatten()
    
    # Append the flattened image and label to the respective lists
    images_list.append(img_array)
    labels_list.append(row['symbol_id'])

# Convert the lists to numpy arrays
X = np.array(images_list)  # Input data of shape (1020, 1024)
y = np.array(labels_list)  # Labels of shape (1020,)

# Check the shapes to ensure correctness
print("Shape of input data (X):", X.shape)
print("Shape of labels (y):", y.shape)

25/23:
import pandas as pd
import os

# Path to the CSV file and image folder

# Read the labels CSV
labels_df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)

# Display basic information about the dataset
print("Total number of rows in CSV:", labels_df.shape[0])
print(labels_df.head())  # Display the first few rows to inspect the structure

# Check the distribution of symbol_id values
symbol_counts = labels_df['symbol_id'].value_counts().sort_index()
print("\nDistribution of symbol_id values:")
print(symbol_counts)

# Filter rows where symbol_id is between 70 and 79 (inclusive)
filtered_labels_df = labels_df[(labels_df['symbol_id'] >= 70) & (labels_df['symbol_id'] <= 79)]
print("\nNumber of rows with symbol_id between 70 and 79:", filtered_labels_df.shape[0])

# Initialize a list to track missing images
missing_images = []

# Loop through the filtered data and check if the image exists
for index, row in filtered_labels_df.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join(image_folder, row['path'])
    
    if not os.path.exists(image_path):
        missing_images.append(image_path)

# Report missing images, if any
print("\nNumber of missing images:", len(missing_images))
if len(missing_images) > 0:
    print("Missing image paths:")
    for img in missing_images:
        print(img)
else:
    print("No missing images. All image paths are valid.")

# Import necessary libraries
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import os

# Path to the CSV file and image folder
csv_path = r'C:\Users\S\Downloads\New folder (8)\hasy-data-labels.csv'
image_folder = r'C:\Users\S\Downloads\New folder (8)\hasy-data\\'

# Read the labels CSV
labels_df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)

# Filter rows where symbol_id is between 70 and 79 (inclusive)
filtered_labels_df = labels_df[(labels_df['symbol_id'] >= 70) & (labels_df['symbol_id'] <= 79)]

# Initialize lists to store the flattened images and corresponding labels
images_list = []
labels_list = []

# Process each image corresponding to the filtered labels
for index, row in filtered_labels_df.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join(image_folder, row['path'])
    
    # Open the image, convert to grayscale (if necessary), and resize to 32x32
    img = Image.open(image_path).convert('L')  # Convert to grayscale ('L' mode)
    
    # Flatten the image into a 1D vector (32x32 = 1024)
    img_array = np.array(img).flatten()
    
    # Append the flattened image and label to the respective lists
    images_list.append(img_array)
    labels_list.append(row['symbol_id'])

# Convert the lists to numpy arrays
X = np.array(images_list)  # Input data of shape (1020, 1024)
y = np.array(labels_list)  # Labels of shape (1020,)

# Check the shapes to ensure correctness
print("Shape of input data (X):", X.shape)
print("Shape of labels (y):", y.shape)

25/31:
import pandas as pd
import os
from PIL import Image
import numpy as np

# Step 1: Read the labels file
labels_file = 'hasy-data-labels.csv'
labels_df = pd.read_csv(labels_file)

# Step 2: Filter rows where symbol_id is between 70 and 79
filtered_labels_df = labels_df[(labels_df['symbol_id'] >= 70) & (labels_df['symbol_id'] <= 79)]

# Step 3: Initialize lists to hold image data and labels
image_data = []
labels = []

# Step 4: Process each image
for index, row in filtered_labels_df.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join('hasy_data', row['path'])  # Adjust the folder name if necessary
    img = Image.open(image_path).convert('L')  # Convert image to black and white (grayscale)
    img_resized = img.resize((32, 32))  # Resize to 32x32
    img_flattened = np.array(img_resized).flatten()  # Flatten the image to a vector
    image_data.append(img_flattened)
    labels.append(row['symbol_id'])

# Step 5: Convert lists to numpy arrays
X = np.array(image_data)
y = np.array(labels)

# Check the shape of the dataset
print(f"Shape of input data (X): {X.shape}")
print(f"Shape of labels (y): {y.shape}")

import pandas as pd
import os
from PIL import Image
import numpy as np

# Step 1: Read the labels file
labels_file = 'hasy-data-labels.csv'
labels_df = pd.read_csv(labels_file)

# Step 2: Filter rows where symbol_id is between 70 and 79
filtered_labels_df = labels_df[(labels_df['symbol_id'] >= 70) & (labels_df['symbol_id'] <= 79)]

# Step 3: Initialize lists to hold image data and labels
# Step 3: Initialize lists to hold image data and labels
image_data = []
labels = []

# Check your directory and adjust the path accordingly
base_image_folder = 'hasy_data'  # Adjust if the folder has a different name or is located elsewhere

# Step 4: Process each image
for index, row in filtered_labels_df.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join(base_image_folder, row['path'])  # Adjust the folder name if necessary
    
    # Print out the image path to debug
    print(f"Attempting to open image: {image_path}")
    
    try:
        # Open and process the image
        img = Image.open(image_path).convert('L')  # Convert image to black and white (grayscale)
        img_resized = img.resize((32, 32))  # Resize to 32x32
        img_flattened = np.array(img_resized).flatten()  # Flatten the image to a vector
        image_data.append(img_flattened)
        labels.append(row['symbol_id'])
    except FileNotFoundError:
        print(f"Image file not found: {image_path}")
        continue  # Skip this image and go to the next one

# Step 5: Convert lists to numpy arrays
X = np.array(image_data)
y = np.array(labels)

# Check the shape of the dataset
print(f"Shape of input data (X): {X.shape}")
print(f"Shape of labels (y): {y.shape}")

import pandas as pd
import os
from PIL import Image
import numpy as np

# Step 1: Read the labels file
labels_file = 'hasy-data-labels.csv'
labels_df = pd.read_csv(labels_file)

# Step 2: Filter rows where symbol_id is between 70 and 79
filtered_labels_df = labels_df[(labels_df['symbol_id'] >= 70) & (labels_df['symbol_id'] <= 79)]

# Step 3: Initialize lists to hold image data and labels
# Step 3: Initialize lists to hold image data and labels
image_data = []
labels = []

# Check your directory and adjust the path accordingly
base_image_folder = r'C:\Users\S\Downloads\New folder (8)\hasy-data'  # Adjust for your operating system

# Step 4: Process each image
for index, row in filtered_labels_df.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join(base_image_folder, row['path'])  # Adjust the folder name if necessary
    
    # Print out the image path to debug
    print(f"Attempting to open image: {image_path}")
    
    try:
        # Open and process the image
        img = Image.open(image_path).convert('L')  # Convert image to black and white (grayscale)
        img_resized = img.resize((32, 32))  # Resize to 32x32
        img_flattened = np.array(img_resized).flatten()  # Flatten the image to a vector
        image_data.append(img_flattened)
        labels.append(row['symbol_id'])
    except FileNotFoundError:
        print(f"Image file not found: {image_path}")
        continue  # Skip this image and go to the next one

# Step 5: Convert lists to numpy arrays

y = np.array(labels)

    # Print out the image path to debug

26/3:
import os
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
from PIL import Image

# Path to the HASYv2 dataset directory
dataset_path = "C:/Users/S/Downloads/New folder (8)/hasy-data"  # Adjust the path as needed

# Load the labels CSV file
labels_df = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(dataset_path, "hasy-data-labels.csv"))

# Filter the labels based on symbol_id range
filtered_labels = labels_df[(labels_df["symbol_id"] >= 70) & (labels_df["symbol_id"] <= 79)]

# Initialize empty lists for images and labels
images = []
labels = []

# Iterate over the filtered labels and load corresponding images
for index, row in filtered_labels.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join(dataset_path, "hasy_data", f"hs{row['image_id']}.png")
    try:
        image = Image.open(image_path).convert("L")  # Convert to grayscale
        image_array = np.array(image).flatten()
        images.append(image_array)
        labels.append(row["symbol_id"])
    except FileNotFoundError:
        print(f"Image file not found: {image_path}")

# Convert lists to NumPy arrays
images = np.array(images)
labels = np.array(labels)

print("Images shape:", images.shape)
print("Labels shape:", labels.shape)
27/1:
# Importing necessary libraries
import geopandas as gpd
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

# Load the world and cities shapefiles
world = gpd.read_file("world_m.shp")
cities = gpd.read_file("cities.shp")

# Check the CRS (Coordinate Reference System) of both datasets
print("World CRS:", world.crs)
print("Cities CRS:", cities.crs)



# Plot the world and cities datasets together
fig, ax = plt.subplots(figsize=(10, 10))
world.plot(ax=ax, color='lightblue', edgecolor='black')  # Plot world with light blue color
cities.plot(ax=ax, color='red', markersize=5)  # Plot cities with red color

# Add a title and show the plot
plt.title('World Map with Cities')
plt.show()

34/3:
import os
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image

base_folder = r"C:\Users\S\Downloads\himan\hasy-data"
csv_path = os.path.join(base_folder, 'hasy-data-labels.csv')
image_folder = os.path.join(base_folder)

df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)
df = df[(df['symbol_id'] >= 70) & (df['symbol_id'] <= 79)]
img_data = []
targets = []
print(df['path'].head())  # Check what the image paths look like

for index, row in df.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join(base_folder, row['path'])
    try:

        print(f"Trying to open image: {image_path}")
        img = Image.open(image_path)
        img = img.convert("L")
        img = img.resize((32, 32))
        img = np.array(img).flatten()
        img_data.append(img)
        targets.append(row['symbol_id'])
    except FileNotFoundError:
        print(f"Image not found: {image_path}")

img_data = np.array(img_data)
targets = np.array(targets)

print('Image data shape = ', img_data.shape)
print('Target vector length = ', targets.shape)

import os
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image

base_folder = r"C:\Users\S\Downloads\himan\hasy-data"
csv_path = os.path.join(base_folder, 'hasy-data-labels.csv')
image_folder = os.path.join(base_folder)

df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)
df = df[(df['symbol_id'] >= 70) & (df['symbol_id'] <= 79)]
img_data = []
targets = []
print(df['path'].head())  # Check what the image paths look like

for index, row in df.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join(base_folder, row['path'])
    try:

        print(f"Trying to open image: {image_path}")
        img = Image.open(image_path)
        img = img.convert("L")
        img = img.resize((32, 32))
        img = np.array(img).flatten()
        img_data.append(img)
        targets.append(row['symbol_id'])
    except FileNotFoundError:
        print(f"Image not found: {image_path}")

img_data = np.array(img_data)
targets = np.array(targets)

print('Image data shape = ', img_data.shape)
print('Target vector length = ', targets.shape)
34/6:
import os
import pandas as pd
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image

base_folder = r"C:\Users\S\Downloads\himan\hasy-data"
csv_path = os.path.join(base_folder, 'hasy-data-labels.csv')
image_folder = os.path.join(base_folder)

df = pd.read_csv(csv_path)
df = df[(df['symbol_id'] >= 70) & (df['symbol_id'] <= 79)]
img_data = []
targets = []
print(df['path'].head())  # Check what the image paths look like

for index, row in df.iterrows():
    image_path = os.path.join(base_folder, row['path'])
    try:

        print(f"Trying to open image: {image_path}")
        img = Image.open(image_path)
        img = img.convert("L")
        img = img.resize((32, 32))
        img = np.array(img).flatten()
        img_data.append(img)
        targets.append(row['symbol_id'])
    except FileNotFoundError:
        print(f"Image not found: {image_path}")

img_data = np.array(img_data)
targets = np.array(targets)

print('Image data shape = ', img_data.shape)
print('Target vector length = ', targets.shape)

import pandas as pd 


infulenza_data = pd.read_csv("data_archive/FlunetData_India_All Sites_for_09 September 1996 to 09 September 2024.csv")
infulenza_data['Week start date (ISO 8601 calendar)'] = pd.to_datetime(infulenza_data['Week start date (ISO 8601 calendar)'])

infulenza_data['Year'] = infulenza_data['Week start date (ISO 8601 calendar)'].dt.year
year_counts = infulenza_data['Year'].value_counts().sort_index()

print("Years and counts (how many datarows within the year):")
print(year_counts)

39/1:

import json
import getpass
import hashlib

def import_pandas_safely():
    try:
        return __import__('pandas')
    except ImportError:
        return False


__pandas = import_pandas_safely()


def is_data_frame(v: str):
    obj = eval(v)
    if  isinstance(obj, __pandas.core.frame.DataFrame) or isinstance(obj, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return True


def dataframe_columns(var):
    df = eval(var)
    if isinstance(df, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return [[df.name, str(df.dtype)]]
    return list(map(lambda col: [col, str(df[col].dtype)], df.columns))


def dtypes_str(frame):
    return str(eval(frame).dtypes)

def dataframe_hash(var):
    # Return a hash including the column names and number of rows
    df = eval(var)
    if isinstance(df, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return hashlib.sha256(f"{var}-{df.name},{len(df)}".encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()
    return hashlib.sha256(f"{var}-{','.join(df.columns)},{len(df)}".encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()

def get_dataframes():
    if __pandas is None:
        return []
    user = getpass.getuser()
    values = %who_ls
    dataframes = [
        {
            "name": var,
            "type": type(eval(var)).__name__,
            "hash": dataframe_hash(var),
            "cols": dataframe_columns(var),
            "dtypesStr": dtypes_str(var),
        }
        for var in values if is_data_frame(var)
    ]
    result = {"dataframes": dataframes, "user": user}
    return json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False)


get_dataframes()

print(year_counts)

import json
import getpass
import hashlib

def import_pandas_safely():
    try:
        return __import__('pandas')
    except ImportError:
        return False


__pandas = import_pandas_safely()


def is_data_frame(v: str):

import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

# Flu cases and temperature trends for Chennai
plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))
plt.plot(merged_chennai['Date'], merged_chennai['Influenza positive'], label='Flu Cases', color='red')
plt.plot(merged_chennai['Date'], merged_chennai['tavg'], label='Average Temperature', color='blue')
plt.xlabel('Date')
plt.ylabel('Value')
plt.title('Flu Cases and Average Temperature in Chennai Over Time')
plt.legend()
plt.show()

# Flu cases and temperature trends for Delhi
plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))
plt.plot(merged_delhi['Date'], merged_delhi['Influenza positive'], label='Flu Cases', color='red')
plt.plot(merged_delhi['Date'], merged_delhi['tavg'], label='Average Temperature', color='blue')
plt.xlabel('Date')
plt.ylabel('Value')
plt.title('Flu Cases and Average Temperature in Delhi Over Time')
plt.legend()
plt.show()

# Flu cases and temperature trends for Jodhpur
plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))
plt.plot(merged_jodhpur['Date'], merged_jodhpur['Influenza positive'], label='Flu Cases', color='red')
plt.plot(merged_jodhpur['Date'], merged_jodhpur['tavg'], label='Average Temperature', color='blue')

import json
import getpass
import hashlib

def import_pandas_safely():
    try:
        return __import__('pandas')
    except ImportError:
        return False


__pandas = import_pandas_safely()


def is_data_frame(v: str):
    obj = eval(v)
    if  isinstance(obj, __pandas.core.frame.DataFrame) or isinstance(obj, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return True


def dataframe_columns(var):
    df = eval(var)
    if isinstance(df, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return [[df.name, str(df.dtype)]]
    return list(map(lambda col: [col, str(df[col].dtype)], df.columns))


def dtypes_str(frame):
    return str(eval(frame).dtypes)

def dataframe_hash(var):
    # Return a hash including the column names and number of rows
    df = eval(var)
    if isinstance(df, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return hashlib.sha256(f"{var}-{df.name},{len(df)}".encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()
    return hashlib.sha256(f"{var}-{','.join(df.columns)},{len(df)}".encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()

def get_dataframes():
    if __pandas is None:
        return []
    user = getpass.getuser()
    values = %who_ls
    dataframes = [
        {
            "name": var,
            "type": type(eval(var)).__name__,
            "hash": dataframe_hash(var),
            "cols": dataframe_columns(var),
            "dtypesStr": dtypes_str(var),
        }
        for var in values if is_data_frame(var)
    ]
    result = {"dataframes": dataframes, "user": user}
    return json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False)


get_dataframes()

39/55:

import json
import getpass
import hashlib

def import_pandas_safely():
    try:
        return __import__('pandas')
    except ImportError:
        return False


__pandas = import_pandas_safely()


def is_data_frame(v: str):
    obj = eval(v)
    if  isinstance(obj, __pandas.core.frame.DataFrame) or isinstance(obj, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return True


def dataframe_columns(var):
    df = eval(var)
    if isinstance(df, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return [[df.name, str(df.dtype)]]
    return list(map(lambda col: [col, str(df[col].dtype)], df.columns))


def dtypes_str(frame):
    return str(eval(frame).dtypes)

def dataframe_hash(var):
    # Return a hash including the column names and number of rows
    df = eval(var)
    if isinstance(df, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return hashlib.sha256(f"{var}-{df.name},{len(df)}".encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()
    return hashlib.sha256(f"{var}-{','.join(df.columns)},{len(df)}".encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()

def get_dataframes():
    if __pandas is None:
        return []
    user = getpass.getuser()
    values = %who_ls
    dataframes = [
        {
            "name": var,
            "type": type(eval(var)).__name__,
            "hash": dataframe_hash(var),
            "cols": dataframe_columns(var),
            "dtypesStr": dtypes_str(var),
        }
        for var in values if is_data_frame(var)
    ]
    result = {"dataframes": dataframes, "user": user}
    return json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False)


get_dataframes()

39/72:

import json
import getpass
import hashlib

def import_pandas_safely():
    try:
        return __import__('pandas')
    except ImportError:
        return False


__pandas = import_pandas_safely()


def is_data_frame(v: str):
    obj = eval(v)
    if  isinstance(obj, __pandas.core.frame.DataFrame) or isinstance(obj, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return True


def dataframe_columns(var):
    df = eval(var)
    if isinstance(df, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return [[df.name, str(df.dtype)]]
    return list(map(lambda col: [col, str(df[col].dtype)], df.columns))


def dtypes_str(frame):
    return str(eval(frame).dtypes)

def dataframe_hash(var):
    # Return a hash including the column names and number of rows
    df = eval(var)
    if isinstance(df, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return hashlib.sha256(f"{var}-{df.name},{len(df)}".encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()
    return hashlib.sha256(f"{var}-{','.join(df.columns)},{len(df)}".encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()

def get_dataframes():
    if __pandas is None:
        return []
    user = getpass.getuser()
    values = %who_ls
    dataframes = [
        {
            "name": var,
            "type": type(eval(var)).__name__,
            "hash": dataframe_hash(var),
            "cols": dataframe_columns(var),
            "dtypesStr": dtypes_str(var),
        }
        for var in values if is_data_frame(var)
    ]
    result = {"dataframes": dataframes, "user": user}
    return json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False)


get_dataframes()

39/109:

import json
import getpass
import hashlib

def import_pandas_safely():
    try:
        return __import__('pandas')
    except ImportError:
        return False


__pandas = import_pandas_safely()


def is_data_frame(v: str):
    obj = eval(v)
    if  isinstance(obj, __pandas.core.frame.DataFrame) or isinstance(obj, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return True


def dataframe_columns(var):
    df = eval(var)
    if isinstance(df, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return [[df.name, str(df.dtype)]]
    return list(map(lambda col: [col, str(df[col].dtype)], df.columns))


def dtypes_str(frame):
    return str(eval(frame).dtypes)

def dataframe_hash(var):
    # Return a hash including the column names and number of rows
    df = eval(var)
    if isinstance(df, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return hashlib.sha256(f"{var}-{df.name},{len(df)}".encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()
    return hashlib.sha256(f"{var}-{','.join(df.columns)},{len(df)}".encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()

def get_dataframes():
    if __pandas is None:
        return []
    user = getpass.getuser()
    values = %who_ls
    dataframes = [
        {
            "name": var,
            "type": type(eval(var)).__name__,
            "hash": dataframe_hash(var),
            "cols": dataframe_columns(var),
            "dtypesStr": dtypes_str(var),
        }
        for var in values if is_data_frame(var)
    ]
    result = {"dataframes": dataframes, "user": user}
    return json.dumps(result, ensure_ascii=False)


get_dataframes()

import seaborn as sns
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt

# Function to plot heatmap for correlation
def plot_heatmap(correlation_matrix, city_name):
    plt.figure(figsize=(8, 6))
    sns.heatmap(correlation_matrix, annot=True, cmap='coolwarm', linewidths=0.5)
    plt.title(f'Correlation between Flu Cases and Weather in {city_name}')
    plt.show()

# Plot heatmaps for each city
plot_heatmap(correlation_chennai, 'Chennai')
plot_heatmap(correlation_delhi, 'Delhi')
plot_heatmap(correlation_jodhpur, 'Jodhpur')
plot_heatmap(correlation_rourkela, 'Rourkela')
plot_heatmap(correlation_bhubaneswar, 'Bhubaneswar')
plot_heatmap(correlation_lucknow, 'Lucknow')
plot_heatmap(correlation_mumbai, 'Mumbai')
plot_heatmap(correlation_bangalore, 'Bangalore')

    if isinstance(df, __pandas.core.series.Series):
        return hashlib.sha256(f"{var}-{df.name},{len(df)}".encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()
    return hashlib.sha256(f"{var}-{','.join(df.columns)},{len(df)}".encode('utf-8')).hexdigest()

    if __pandas is None:
        return []
    user = getpass.getuser()
    values = %who_ls
    dataframes = [
        {
            "name": var,
            "type": type(eval(var)).__name__,
